````text
## How does LiteLLM handle a request?[​](#how-it-works "Direct link to How does LiteLLM handle a request?")

LiteLLM handles a request in one of three ways. The diagram shows each path from your app to the provider.

Three request paths through LiteLLM Gateway. Translated: your app sends a chat request in the OpenAI, Anthropic, or Google format, and LiteLLM maps it to any chat provider. Native: your app calls an endpoint such as embeddings, images, speech, or rerank with one request shape, and LiteLLM maps it to each provider of that endpoint. Pass-through: your app calls the API of one provider through LiteLLM, which adds the provider credentials and forwards the request unchanged.

Your app sendsLiteLLM GatewayProviders

TranslatedOne chat request, in the format your app already uses.

LiteLLM GatewayMaps the format

`POST /v1/chat/completions`OpenAI format

`POST /v1/responses`OpenAI format

`POST /v1/messages`Anthropic format

`generateContent`Google format

Anthropic, Bedrock, Azure OpenAI, Gemini, and any other chat provider

The answer comes back in the format your app sent.

NativeOne request shape for each endpoint.

LiteLLM GatewayMaps to each provider API

`POST /v1/embeddings`

OpenAI, Bedrock, Cohere, Mistral, and more

`POST /v1/images/generations`

OpenAI, Bedrock, Gemini, Stability AI, and more

`POST /v1/audio/speech`

OpenAI, Azure OpenAI, AWS Polly, ElevenLabs, and more

`POST /v1/rerank`

Cohere, Bedrock, Jina AI, Voyage AI, and more

The same request works for every provider of that endpoint.

Pass-throughThe API of one provider, called through LiteLLM.

LiteLLM GatewayAdds provider credentials, forwards unchanged

`/anthropic/v1/messages`

Anthropic

`/bedrock/...`

Bedrock

`/gemini/...`

Google AI Studio

`/cohere/...`

Cohere

Use it for a provider feature that LiteLLM does not map.

Takes a chat request in one of three formats and maps it to the API of any chat provider

Takes one request shape for each endpoint and maps it to the API of each provider that supports it

Sends your request to the provider API without changes

**Request format**

OpenAI, Anthropic, or Google

Usually OpenAI. Rerank uses the Cohere format, search the Perplexity format, and OCR the Mistral format

The API of the provider

**Examples**

`/v1/chat/completions`, `/v1/responses`, `/v1/messages`, `generateContent`

`/v1/embeddings`, `/v1/images/generations`, `/v1/audio/speech`, `/v1/rerank`

`/anthropic/v1/messages`, `/bedrock/...`, `/gemini/...`

**LiteLLM keys and logs**

Yes

Yes

Yes

**Cost tracking**

Yes

Yes

For the providers that support it. Each provider page says if it does

**Use it when**

You want one client for many models

You use one capability across providers

You already use the provider SDK, or you need a provider feature that LiteLLM does not map

## Which providers have pass-through endpoints?[​](#pass-through-endpoints "Direct link to Which providers have pass-through endpoints?")

A pass-through route sends a request to the API of one provider. LiteLLM checks your LiteLLM key, adds the provider credentials, and logs the request. Cost tracking depends on the provider. Read [why to use pass-through endpoints](/docs/pass_through/intro), or select a provider.

## Frequently asked questions[​](#faq "Direct link to Frequently asked questions")

### Which endpoints does LiteLLM support?[​](#which-endpoints-does-litellm-support "Direct link to Which endpoints does LiteLLM support?")

LiteLLM supports more than 40 endpoints. They include chat completions, responses, Anthropic messages, embeddings, images, video, audio, realtime, rerank, vector stores, files, batches, MCP, and A2A. This page lists them, with a link to the guide for each.

### Can I send an Anthropic-format request to a model that is not from Anthropic?[​](#can-i-send-an-anthropic-format-request-to-a-model-that-is-not-from-anthropic "Direct link to Can I send an Anthropic-format request to a model that is not from Anthropic?")

Yes. Send the request to `/v1/messages` in the Anthropic format and set the model to any chat model in your config. LiteLLM changes the request to the API of that provider and sends the answer back in the Anthropic format. See [/v1/messages](/docs/anthropic_unified/).

### Can I call a model that only supports the Responses API from /chat/completions?[​](#can-i-call-a-model-that-only-supports-the-responses-api-from-chatcompletions "Direct link to Can I call a model that only supports the Responses API from /chat/completions?")

Yes. LiteLLM bridges `/chat/completions` to the Responses API when the provider does not support chat completions for that model. The reverse also works. `/v1/responses` can call providers that only have chat completions. See [/responses](/docs/response_api).

### What is the difference between a translated endpoint and a pass-through endpoint?[​](#what-is-the-difference-between-a-translated-endpoint-and-a-pass-through-endpoint "Direct link to What is the difference between a translated endpoint and a pass-through endpoint?")

A translated endpoint takes one request format and calls many providers. A pass-through endpoint sends your request to the API of one provider without changes. Both use your LiteLLM key and are logged. For pass-through endpoints, cost tracking depends on the provider.

### Do the endpoints work in the Python SDK and in the AI Gateway?[​](#do-the-endpoints-work-in-the-python-sdk-and-in-the-ai-gateway "Direct link to Do the endpoints work in the Python SDK and in the AI Gateway?")

Most endpoints have a Python SDK function and a Gateway route. Each card on this page shows the Python function when one exists. Serving MCP servers is a Gateway feature only.

### How do I ask for an endpoint that is not on this page?[​](#how-do-i-ask-for-an-endpoint-that-is-not-on-this-page "Direct link to How do I ask for an endpoint that is not on this page?")

[Open an issue](https://github.com/BerriAI/litellm/issues/new) in the BerriAI/litellm repository on GitHub with the endpoint and the providers you need.
````

````text
# Token & Token Usage

Tokens are the basic units used by models to represent natural language text, and also the units we use for billing. They can be intuitively understood as 'characters' or 'words'. Typically, a Chinese word, an English word, a number, or a symbol is counted as a token.

Generally, the conversion ratio between tokens in the model and the number of characters is approximately as following:

-   1 English character ≈ 0.3 token.
-   1 Chinese character ≈ 0.6 token.

However, due to the different tokenization methods used by different models, the conversion ratios can vary. The actual number of tokens processed each time is based on the model's return, which you can view from the usage results.

## Calculate token usage offline[​](#calculate-token-usage-offline "Direct link to Calculate token usage offline")

You can run the demo tokenizer code in the following zip package to calculate the token usage for your input/output.

[deepseek\_tokenizer.zip](https://cdn.deepseek.com/api-docs/deepseek_v4_tokenizer.zip)

## Calculate image token usage[​](#calculate-image-token-usage "Direct link to Calculate image token usage")

You can estimate the number of tokens consumed by an image based on its dimensions. Images are automatically resized before inference, and there is an upper bound on the number of tokens per image; for details, see [Vision](/guides/vision#token-usage).

This is an estimate only; the actual number of tokens produced during processing may vary slightly, so refer to the usage returned by the API as the source of truth.
````

### Message 83 — assistant — 2026-11-22 10:12:38 UTC

This is significant. Two official facts change the plan:

1. **litellm exposes `POST /v1/responses/input_tokens`** ("Token Counting ... before you send a request"). Our `405` was likely a *method/path* mismatch, not proven absence. The SDK sends `POST /v1/responses/input_tokens/count` (with `/count`) — a different path.
2. **DeepSeek publishes an official offline tokenizer** (`deepseek_v4_tokenizer.zip`) — the exact model's tokenizer, which is the defensible way to pre-count.

Let me verify the litellm count route's exact path and request shape (read-only).

````text
# Token Counting

## Overview[​](#overview "Direct link to Overview")

LiteLLM provides exact token counting by calling provider-specific token counting APIs. This gives you accurate token counts before sending requests, helping with cost estimation and context window management.

Feature

Details

SDK Method

`litellm.acount_tokens()`

Proxy Endpoints

`/v1/messages/count_tokens` (Anthropic format), `/v1/responses/input_tokens` (OpenAI format)

Local count

The SDK and the providers that have no count API use a local count. On the proxy, a failed provider count returns an error. Refer to [Proxy errors](#proxy-errors-instead-of-local-estimates)

## SDK Usage[​](#sdk-usage "Direct link to SDK Usage")

### Basic Usage[​](#basic-usage "Direct link to Basic Usage")

```
import asyncioimport litellmasync def main():    # OpenAI    result = await litellm.acount_tokens(        model="openai/gpt-6.1-sol",        messages=[{"role": "user", "content": "Hello, how are you?"}],    )    print(f"Token count: {result.total_tokens}")    print(f"Tokenizer: {result.tokenizer_type}")  # "openai_api"    # Anthropic    result = await litellm.acount_tokens(        model="anthropic/claude-sonnet-5",        messages=[{"role": "user", "content": "Hello, how are you?"}],    )    print(f"Token count: {result.total_tokens}")    print(f"Tokenizer: {result.tokenizer_type}")  # "anthropic_api"    # Mistral    result = await litellm.acount_tokens(        model="mistral/mistral-large-4",        messages=[{"role": "user", "content": "Hello, how are you?"}],    )    print(f"Token count: {result.total_tokens}")    print(f"Tokenizer: {result.tokenizer_type}")  # "mistral_api"asyncio.run(main())
```

### With Tools and System Message[​](#with-tools-and-system-message "Direct link to With Tools and System Message")

```
import asyncioimport litellmasync def main():    result = await litellm.acount_tokens(        model="openai/gpt-6.1-sol",        messages=[{"role": "user", "content": "What's the weather in Paris?"}],        tools=[{            "type": "function",            "function": {                "name": "get_weather",                "description": "Get weather for a city",                "parameters": {                    "type": "object",                    "properties": {"city": {"type": "string"}},                },            },        }],        system="You are a helpful weather assistant.",    )    print(f"Token count (with tools): {result.total_tokens}")asyncio.run(main())
```

### Response Format[​](#response-format "Direct link to Response Format")

`litellm.acount_tokens()` returns a `TokenCountResponse`:

```
TokenCountResponse(    total_tokens=15,           # Token count    request_model="openai/gpt-6.1-sol",  # Model requested    model_used="gpt-6.1-sol",      # Model used for counting    tokenizer_type="openai_api",    # "openai_api", "anthropic_api", "mistral_api", "bedrock_api", "bedrock_mantle_api", "local_tokenizer"    original_response={"input_tokens": 15},  # Raw API response    error=False,               # True if counting failed    error_message=None,        # Error details if failed)
```

### SDK Fallback Behavior[​](#sdk-fallback-behavior "Direct link to SDK Fallback Behavior")

In the SDK, `acount_tokens()` uses a local tiktoken count if the provider has no count API or if the API key is missing. The proxy count routes do not do this. They return an error. Refer to [Proxy errors instead of local estimates](#proxy-errors-instead-of-local-estimates).

```
# Unsupported provider → automatic fallbackresult = await litellm.acount_tokens(    model="together_ai/meta-llama/Llama-3-8b-chat-hf",    messages=[{"role": "user", "content": "Hello"}],)print(result.tokenizer_type)  # "local_tokenizer"
```

On the proxy, local counting runs in a worker thread, so a large payload does not hold up other requests. Each worker process counts at most `TOKEN_COUNTER_MAX_CONCURRENT_COUNTS` payloads at a time (default 4) and queues the rest, which bounds the memory a burst of large counts can take. Strings longer than `TOKEN_COUNTER_MAX_EXACT_CHARS` characters (default 4,000,000, roughly a million tokens) are estimated by tokenizing 16 evenly spaced samples that together total that many characters and scaling the result by the string's length, which keeps the cost of the largest payloads bounded.

### Bedrock Claude models[​](#bedrock-claude-models "Direct link to Bedrock Claude models")

The bedrock-runtime CountTokens API rejects some Claude models with a 400 (Claude Opus 4.8, 5 and 5.5 when this was written). For a Claude model it rejects, LiteLLM sends the same body to bedrock-mantle (`https://bedrock-mantle.<region>.api.aws/anthropic/v1/messages/count_tokens`), signed with the deployment's AWS credentials, and reports `tokenizer_type: "bedrock_mantle_api"`. A model bedrock-runtime counts never reaches Mantle and keeps `tokenizer_type: "bedrock_api"`

The credentials need the IAM action `bedrock-mantle:CountTokens` next to `bedrock:CountTokens`. Without it, Mantle returns a 403. The SDK then uses the local tokenizer. The proxy count routes return the 403. A Bedrock API key (`AWS_BEARER_TOKEN_BEDROCK`) is sent to Mantle as the bearer token, so the same policy applies to it

Set `BEDROCK_MANTLE_API_BASE` to send the Mantle call to another host, for example a VPC endpoint. The deployment's `api_base` and `aws_bedrock_runtime_endpoint` apply to bedrock-runtime only. For a non-Claude model such as Amazon Nova, or a Claude model that Mantle does not serve in the region, the SDK uses a local count. The proxy returns the 400 or 404 from AWS. If the deployment sets [`token_count_framing` or another framing key](#local-estimates-for-models-without-a-count-api), the proxy returns a local estimate. If the region has no Mantle host, the proxy returns a 500

## Proxy Usage[​](#proxy-usage "Direct link to Proxy Usage")

### OpenAI Format: `/v1/responses/input_tokens`[​](#openai-format-v1responsesinput_tokens "Direct link to openai-format-v1responsesinput_tokens")

-   curl
-   Python (httpx)

```
curl -X POST "http://localhost:4000/v1/responses/input_tokens" \  -H "Content-Type: application/json" \  -H "Authorization: Bearer $LITELLM_API_KEY" \  -d '{    "model": "gpt-6.1-sol",    "input": "Hello, how are you?"  }'
```

```
import httpxresponse = httpx.post(    "http://localhost:4000/v1/responses/input_tokens",    headers={        "Content-Type": "application/json",        "Authorization": "Bearer sk-<your-litellm-api-key>"    },    json={        "model": "gpt-6.1-sol",        "input": "Hello, how are you?"    })print(response.json())# {"object": "response.input_tokens", "input_tokens": 13}
```

**Response:**

```
{"object": "response.input_tokens", "input_tokens": 13}
```

### Anthropic Format: `/v1/messages/count_tokens`[​](#anthropic-format-v1messagescount_tokens "Direct link to anthropic-format-v1messagescount_tokens")

See [Anthropic Token Counting](/docs/anthropic_count_tokens) for full documentation.

```
curl -X POST "http://localhost:4000/v1/messages/count_tokens" \  -H "Content-Type: application/json" \  -H "Authorization: Bearer $LITELLM_API_KEY" \  -d '{    "model": "claude-sonnet-5",    "messages": [      {"role": "user", "content": "Hello, how are you?"}    ]  }'
```

### Proxy errors instead of local estimates[​](#proxy-errors-instead-of-local-estimates "Direct link to Proxy errors instead of local estimates")

From v1.108.0, the proxy count routes do not return a local tiktoken estimate when they cannot use the count from the provider. Before v1.108.0, the routes returned a 200 with this estimate. For some models, the estimate was very different from the correct count. The proxy count routes are:

-   `/v1/messages/count_tokens`
-   `/v1/responses/input_tokens`
-   Google `:countTokens`
-   `/utils/token_counter?call_endpoint=true`

Each error uses the error format of the API that you used: Anthropic, OpenAI, or Google.

Condition

Response

The count API of the provider fails, for example because of a bad key, an outage, or a 429

The status and the message from the provider

An OpenAI, Anthropic, or Azure AI deployment has no API key

A 401 that names the missing key

A Bedrock deployment has no AWS credentials

A 500 with the credential error

A Gemini deployment has no API key

The error that Google returns

A Vertex AI partner model, such as GLM, has no count API

A 400 that names the model

A non-Claude Bedrock model, such as Amazon Nova, has no count API

The 400 or 404 from AWS

`litellm.disable_token_counter` is set, and the provider has no count API

A 503

If the deployment sets `token_count_framing` or another framing key, the proxy returns a local estimate in place of a 400 or a 404 from the provider. Refer to [Local estimates for models without a count API](#local-estimates-for-models-without-a-count-api). A Vertex AI partner model with `custom_tokenizer` in `model_info` continues to use a local count and does not return the 400.

This change does not apply to providers with no count API, such as Groq. They continue to use a local count. The SDK function `litellm.acount_tokens()` also continues to use a local count.

### Local estimates for models without a count API[​](#local-estimates-for-models-without-a-count-api "Direct link to Local estimates for models without a count API")

To get a local estimate for a model that the provider cannot count, set `token_count_framing` in the `model_info` of the deployment. The value is a number of tokens. The proxy adds this number to the local estimate for the chat template tokens that the local tokenizer does not see. The proxy also adds it to each local estimate for providers that have no count API. For a request that a narrower framing key covers, the proxy adds the value of that key in its place.

A framing key changes only a 400 or a 404 from the provider into a local estimate. The framing keys are `token_count_framing` and the three keys in [Framing for tool requests and the Responses API](#framing-for-tool-requests-and-the-responses-api). Any one of them turns on the local estimate for every request to the deployment, also for a request that the key does not apply to. On v1.107.0 to v1.109.x, only `token_count_framing` turns it on. For example, from v1.110.0, with only `token_count_tool_framing` set, a chat request with no function tool gets a local estimate with the `token_count_framing` value of the cost map entry, or with no framing when the entry has none. So set `token_count_framing` too. For a 401, 403, 429, or 5xx error, the proxy returns the error. Set the framing keys on each deployment of the model group. A deployment without them continues to return the 400.

As an alternative, set `token_count_model` to count with a different model that the provider can count. Use the keys `model` and `region`. The key `region` is optional. If you set `token_count_model`, a 400 or a 404 from the provider gets a count with that model in place of a local estimate. If the count with `token_count_model` fails, the proxy returns the error. Each local estimate still adds the framing keys, for example for a provider with no count API, or a `/utils/token_counter` request without `call_endpoint=true`.

```
model_list:  - model_name: glm    litellm_params:      model: vertex_ai/zai-org/glm-5-maas    model_info:      token_count_framing: 8  # tokens added to the local estimate  - model_name: claude-sonnet    litellm_params:      model: bedrock/us.anthropic.claude-sonnet-5-5    model_info:      token_count_model:        model: us.anthropic.claude-opus-5-5        region: us-west-2  # replaces the built-in us-east-1 value
```

The built-in cost map sets these values for some models. Examples are Kimi K3 on Bedrock and Databricks, and Claude Sonnet 5.5 and Haiku 5.5 on Bedrock. These models can give a count without `model_info`. A value in `model_info` replaces the built-in value.

The `/v1/messages/count_tokens`, `/v1/responses/input_tokens`, and `:countTokens` responses show the source of the count in `litellm_count_source`. On `/utils/token_counter`, the field is `count_source`. The values are:

-   `provider`: the count API of the provider for the deployment
-   `token_count_model`: the count API for the model in `token_count_model`. The field `litellm_count_model` gives the name of this model.
-   `local_estimate`: a local count

### Framing for tool requests and the Responses API[​](#framing-for-tool-requests-and-the-responses-api "Direct link to Framing for tool requests and the Responses API")

Some hosts add a different hidden prompt when a request has tools, or when it goes to their own Responses API. Three more `model_info` keys set the framing for these requests. Since v1.110.0

Key

Applies to

`token_count_framing`

Every request that a narrower key does not cover

`token_count_tool_framing`

A request with a function tool

`token_count_responses_framing`

A native Responses request

`token_count_responses_tool_framing`

A native Responses request with a function tool

The proxy adds one framing value to each local estimate. A count from the count API of the provider gets no framing, and neither does a `/utils/token_counter` request with only a `prompt`. A narrower key replaces `token_count_framing` and is not added to it. For example, with `token_count_framing: 149` and `token_count_tool_framing: 302`, a request with one function tool adds 302 tokens, not 451.

A function tool is a tool with a name, other than the Anthropic `web_search` tool. This includes OpenAI function tools, Anthropic custom tools, and Anthropic built-in tools such as `web_fetch` and `code_execution`. A request whose only tools are the Anthropic `web_search` tool or tools with no name, such as the Responses `web_search` and `file_search` tools, uses the keys for a request with no function tool.

A native Responses request is a `/v1/responses/input_tokens` request to a deployment for which the proxy calls the provider's own Responses API. When the proxy bridges the deployment to chat completions, the host adds its chat prompt, so the count uses the chat keys. For example, an Azure AI deployment uses the bridge when its `api_base` is not on `.services.ai.azure.com` or `.openai.azure.com`, or when the deployment or the request sets `use_chat_completions_api: true`. If `default_litellm_params` in `router_settings` sets `api_base` or `use_chat_completions_api`, the proxy uses that value in place of the value of the deployment. `/v1/messages/count_tokens` and `/utils/token_counter` always use the chat keys.

When a key is not set, the proxy uses the next key in the list for the request. A chat request is every count request that is not a native Responses request:

Request

Keys, in order

Chat, no function tool

`token_count_framing`

Chat, function tool

`token_count_tool_framing`, `token_count_framing`

Native Responses, no function tool

`token_count_responses_framing`, `token_count_framing`

Native Responses, function tool

`token_count_responses_tool_framing`, `token_count_responses_framing`, `token_count_tool_framing`, `token_count_framing`

If `model_info` sets one or more keys from the list for a request, the proxy uses only the `model_info` keys for that request. If not, it uses the built-in values from the cost map. So `token_count_framing` alone in `model_info` applies to every request and replaces all the built-in values. `token_count_tool_framing` alone applies to tool requests on both APIs, and requests with no function tool keep the built-in values. Each value is a whole number of tokens, zero or more. The proxy ignores any other value.

The local estimate renders tools the way the chat template of the model does, for example for Grok 4.1, Grok 4.3, and DeepSeek V4.1. The proxy finds the model from `base_model` when the deployment sets it in `model_info` or `litellm_params`, and from `litellm_params.model` if not. The built-in framing values come from the cost map entry with the same name, so write `base_model` with its provider prefix, such as `azure_ai/grok-4-1-fast-reasoning`. With `base_model: grok-4-1-fast-reasoning`, the proxy still renders the tools the way Grok 4.1 does, but it finds no cost map entry and adds no framing. So a deployment with a custom name needs `base_model` with the prefix to get the built-in framing values:

```
model_list:  - model_name: prod-grok    litellm_params:      model: azure_ai/prod-grok      api_base: https://my-resource.services.ai.azure.com      api_key: os.environ/AZURE_AI_API_KEY    model_info:      base_model: azure_ai/grok-4-1-fast-reasoning
```

```
curl -X POST "http://localhost:4000/v1/messages/count_tokens" \  -H "Content-Type: application/json" \  -H "Authorization: Bearer $LITELLM_API_KEY" \  -d '{    "model": "prod-grok",    "messages": [{"role": "user", "content": "What is the weather in Paris?"}],    "tools": [{      "name": "get_weather",      "description": "Get the current weather for a city",      "input_schema": {"type": "object", "properties": {"city": {"type": "string"}}, "required": ["city"]}    }]  }'# {"input_tokens": 352, "litellm_count_source": "local_estimate"}
```

Without `base_model`, the same request counts 54 tokens, because the proxy uses the generic tool rendering and no framing. An xAI deployment that still uses a deprecated `grok-4-1` name has no cost map entry, because xAI serves Grok 4.3 for these names. To count it like Grok 4.3, set `base_model: xai/grok-4.3`. `base_model` also changes the other values that the proxy reads from the cost map for the deployment, such as the prices that spend tracking uses. Refer to [Custom pricing](/docs/proxy/custom_pricing).

The cost map has measured framing values for these entries and for others:

Cost map entry

Keys set

`azure_ai/grok-4-1-fast-reasoning`

All four keys

`xai/grok-4.3`

`token_count_framing`, `token_count_tool_framing`

`deepseek/deepseek-v4-flash`

`token_count_framing`, `token_count_tool_framing`

`deepseek/deepseek-v4-pro`

`token_count_framing`, `token_count_tool_framing`

`fireworks_ai/deepseek-v4p1-flash`

`token_count_framing`

`fireworks_ai/accounts/fireworks/models/deepseek-v4p1-flash`

`token_count_framing`

The proxy loads the cost map from GitHub when it starts and on `/reload/model_cost_map`, so these values also reach older proxies. v1.107.0 to v1.109.x and the patch releases from v1.104.4, v1.105.2, and v1.106.1 add the `token_count_framing` value of the entry to every local estimate, tool requests included, and do not read the three newer keys. To use a different value there, set `token_count_framing` in `model_info`, which replaces the built-in value. Older releases do not read any framing key. [`LITELLM_LOCAL_MODEL_COST_MAP=True`](/docs/proxy/config_settings) keeps the cost map of the installed version, but it also keeps the model prices of that version.

```
````

### Message 84 — assistant — 2026-11-22 10:12:40 UTC

This is decisive and reframes everything. Read-only investigation complete — no code changes, no API calls.

# Token-budget dependency: investigation findings

## The gateway is LiteLLM (confirmed)
Our earlier `400` body said `litellm.BadRequestError ... Model Group=CS5351/DeepSeek-V4-Flash`. So `https://llm.cs.cityu.edu.hk/v1` is a **LiteLLM proxy**, not raw Azure/OpenAI. That changes the "absent" conclusions.

## Confirmed (from official docs + local code)
1. **LiteLLM provides a pre-request token-count route:** `POST /v1/responses/input_tokens` (OpenAI format), returning `{"input_tokens": N}`. Docs explicitly: *"Count input tokens ... before you send a request."*
   - **Our `405` was almost certainly a path/method mismatch, not proven absence.** The OpenAI SDK's `responses.input_tokens.count(...)` posts to `.../input_tokens/count`; LiteLLM's documented route is `/v1/responses/input_tokens` (no `/count`). I must not infer the capability is absent from the SDK lacking/using a wrong route.
2. **LiteLLM behavior for models without a provider count API:** the proxy count routes return **either a provider count or a local tokenizer count**, and (≥v1.108.0) return an **error instead of a silent estimate** when it cannot count — *unless* the deployment configures `token_count_framing`. Critically, the docs name **`deepseek/deepseek-v4-flash` and `deepseek/deepseek-v4-pro`** as entries in the cost map with `token_count_framing` + `token_count_tool_framing` set. That means **the local estimate for this exact model includes a measured chat-template framing value** — i.e. a defensible, documented overhead, not an arbitrary one.
3. **DeepSeek publishes an official offline tokenizer** (`deepseek_v4_tokenizer.zip`) for counting input/output offline, with official guidance that *actual usage comes from the API `usage` field*.
4. **Provider-reported usage is authoritative** post-call (`usage.prompt/completion/total`), and our run recorded `260 + 85 = 345` — that is real, but post-hoc.

## Still unknown / must be verified at runtime (not assumable)
- Whether **this** CityUCS LiteLLM instance has the count route enabled and configured for `CS5351/DeepSeek-V4-Flash` (model-group alias, not `deepseek/deepseek-v4-flash`). Alias naming matters for framing lookup (`base_model`).
- Which count source that route returns: **provider API count vs `local_estimate`** — the docs expose this in a field (`litellm_count_source`). This distinction is exactly what determines whether it's "exact" or "estimated."
- Whether the deployment sets `token_count_model`/framing keys for the alias.
- The **correct request path** (`/input_tokens` vs `/input_tokens/count`) and required body (`model` + `input`/`messages`).
- Whether `response_format: json_schema` rejection is permanent (confirmed rejected once) — that's an output concern, separate from counting.

## The exact question for the gateway administrator
Given we cannot confirm from docs alone how *this* instance is configured, the precise question is:

> "For model group `CS5351/DeepSeek-V4-Flash` on `llm.cs.cityu.edu.hk`, (a) is the LiteLLM token-count route `POST /v1/responses/input_tokens` enabled? (b) Does it return a **provider token count** or a **local estimate**, and what does `litellm_count_source` report? (c) Is `token_count_framing` / `token_count_model` set for this alias, and what `base_model` does it map to? (d) What is the accepted request body/path (`input` vs `messages`, `/input_tokens` vs `/input_tokens/count`)?"

If the admin confirms a **provider/exact count** (source `provider`) or a **framed local count** for this alias → we can build a **defensible pre-generation cap** (count + 300 ≤ 3000) and unblock production. If the route is disabled and no tokenizer/framing applies → the cap **cannot** be enforced and we'd need the admin to enable it.

## Implications for the plan (no implementation yet)
The "always-blocked" design is **premature**. The viable defensible path is now:
- **Production `CityUProvider.count_input_tokens`** calls the LiteLLM count route (correct path/body), records `litellm_count_source`, and:
  - treats a **source=`provider`** count as authoritative → enforce `count + 300 ≤ 3000`;
  - treats **source=`local_estimate`** as acceptable **only if** the admin confirms framing for this alias, and labels it an estimate in logs (never silently "exact");
  - on error/unavailable → **fail closed** (block).
- **Offline tokenizer** (DeepSeek's official zip) is a fallback for local framing, but chat-template framing must still be the documented/measured value — not invented.

## What I preserved
No code changes, no API calls. The successful experimental evidence and all existing files (`src/ai/`, `test/live/ai/cityu_experimental.py`, live test, offline tests) are untouched.

````text
<content>
1: """Advisory medical-text extraction (REQ-FR-002, REQ-INV-002, REQ-INV-004)."""
2:
3: import json
4: import logging
5: import math
6: import re
7: from datetime import date
8: from pathlib import Path
9: from typing import Any, Protocol
10:
11: from src.schemas import ExtractedField, ExtractionResult
12:
13: log = logging.getLogger(__name__)
14:
15: # JSON syntax is a subset of YAML 1.2; no additional runtime parser needed.
16: PROMPT = json.loads((Path(__file__).resolve().parents[2] / 'prompts' / 'prompt_v1.0.yaml')
17:                     .read_text(encoding='utf-8'))
18: PROMPT_VERSION: str = PROMPT['version']
19: FIELDS = tuple(ExtractionResult.model_fields)
20: MIN_CONFIDENCE = 0.75
21: MAX_TOTAL_TOKENS = 3000
22: MAX_OUTPUT_TOKENS = 300
23:
24: LABELS = {
25:     'hospital_name': ('hospital', 'hospital name', 'clinic', 'clinic name', '醫院', '医院', '診所', '诊所'),
26:     'visit_date': ('visit date', 'consultation date', '就診日期', '就诊日期', '求診日期'),
27:     'rest_days': ('recommended rest', 'recommended rest days', '建議休息', '建议休息'),
28:     'diagnosis': ('diagnosis', '診斷', '诊断'),
29: }
30:
31:
32: class ExtractionProvider(Protocol):
33:     """Injected adapter: count matching full input, then generate once with an output cap."""
34:
35:     def count_input_tokens(self, *, instructions: str, document: str,
36:                            schema: dict[str, Any]) -> int: ...
37:
38:     def generate(self, *, instructions: str, document: str, schema: dict[str, Any],
39:                  max_output_tokens: int) -> tuple[str, int]: ...
40:
41:
42: def _wire_schema() -> dict[str, Any]:
43:     # Tighten the shared model for this wire path without changing its definitions.
44:     field = {
45:         'type': 'object',
46:         'properties': {
47:             'value': {'type': ['string', 'null']},
48:             'derived': {'type': 'boolean', 'enum': [True]},
49:             'confidence': {'type': 'number'},
50:         },
51:         'required': list(ExtractedField.model_fields),
52:         'additionalProperties': False,
53:     }
54:     return {'type': 'object', 'properties': dict.fromkeys(FIELDS, field),
55:             'required': list(FIELDS), 'additionalProperties': False}
56:
57:
58: def _empty() -> ExtractionResult:
59:     return ExtractionResult(**{key: ExtractedField(value=None, derived=True, confidence=0)
60:                                for key in FIELDS})
61:
62:
63: def _unique(pairs: list[tuple[str, Any]]) -> dict[str, Any]:
64:     result: dict[str, Any] = {}
65:     for key, value in pairs:
66:         if key in result:
67:             raise ValueError('duplicate JSON key')
68:         result[key] = value
69:     return result
70:
71:
72: def _decode(raw: str) -> ExtractionResult:
73:     data = json.loads(raw, object_pairs_hook=_unique)
74:     if type(data) is not dict or data.keys() != set(FIELDS):
75:         raise ValueError('invalid field set')
76:     for field in data.values():
77:         if type(field) is not dict or field.keys() != set(ExtractedField.model_fields):
78:             raise ValueError('invalid derived field shape')
79:         confidence = field['confidence']
80:         value = field['value']
81:         if (field['derived'] is not True or (value is not None and type(value) is not str)
82:                 or type(confidence) not in (int, float) or not 0 <= confidence <= 1
83:                 or not math.isfinite(confidence)):
84:             raise ValueError('invalid derived field values')
85:     return ExtractionResult.model_validate(data, strict=True)
86:
87:
88: def _supported(key: str, value: str, document: str) -> bool:
89:     # Deliberately conservative: only one unique, complete, field-labelled value.
90:     labels = '|'.join(map(re.escape, LABELS[key]))
91:     matches = {match.strip() for match in re.findall(
92:         rf'(?im)^[ \t]*(?:{labels})[ \t]*[:：][ \t]*([^\r\n]*)', document)}
93:     if not value or value != value.strip() or matches != {value}:
94:         return False
95:     if key == 'visit_date':
96:         if not re.fullmatch(r'[0-9]{4}-[0-9]{2}-[0-9]{2}', value):
97:             return False
98:         try:
99:             date.fromisoformat(value)
100:         except ValueError:
101:             return False
102:     if key == 'rest_days' and not re.fullmatch(
103:         r'[1-9][0-9]{0,2}(?:[ \t]*(?:days?|天|日))?', value, re.IGNORECASE
104:     ):
105:         return False
106:     return True
107:
108:
109: def extract(document: str, *, provider: ExtractionProvider, ai_allowed: bool = False,
110:             simple_request: bool = False) -> ExtractionResult:
111:     """Extract from sanitized, redacted text. Core sets ai_allowed only after all prechecks.
112:
113:     Unknown, skipped and failed fields carry null values; this is never a policy decision.
114:     """
115:     if ai_allowed is not True or simple_request or not document.strip():
116:         log.info('ai extraction skipped; tokens=0')
117:         return _empty()
118:
119:     request = {'instructions': PROMPT['instructions'], 'document': document,
120:                'schema': _wire_schema()}
121:     try:
122:         input_tokens = provider.count_input_tokens(**request)
123:     except Exception:
124:         log.warning('ai extraction preflight failed')
125:         return _empty()
126:     if (type(input_tokens) is not int or input_tokens <= 0
127:             or input_tokens + MAX_OUTPUT_TOKENS > MAX_TOTAL_TOKENS):
128:         log.info('ai extraction blocked by token budget')
129:         return _empty()
130:     try:
131:         raw, total = provider.generate(**request, max_output_tokens=MAX_OUTPUT_TOKENS)
132:     except Exception:
133:         log.warning('ai extraction provider failed; usage unknown')
134:         return _empty()
135:     if (type(total) is not int or total <= 0
136:             or total > input_tokens + MAX_OUTPUT_TOKENS):
137:         log.warning('ai extraction provider usage invalid')
138:         return _empty()
139:     log.info('ai extraction tokens=%d prompt=%s', total, PROMPT_VERSION)
140:     try:
141:         parsed = _decode(raw)
142:     except (TypeError, ValueError, RecursionError):
143:         log.warning('ai extraction response invalid')
144:         return _empty()
145:
146:     confirmed = {}
147:     for key in FIELDS:
148:         field = getattr(parsed, key)
149:         if field.value is None or not _supported(key, field.value, document):
150:             field = ExtractedField(value=None, derived=True, confidence=0)
151:         elif field.confidence < MIN_CONFIDENCE:
152:             field = field.model_copy(update={'value': None})
153:         confirmed[key] = field
154:     return ExtractionResult(**confirmed)

(End of file - total 154 lines)
</content>
````

````text
1: """Optional live adapter. No provider contact occurs while importing this module."""
2:
3: from typing import Any
4:
5:
6: class OpenAIProvider:
7:     """Use a pinned structured-output-capable model and an approved API account."""
8:
9:     def __init__(self, client: Any, *, model_version: str):
10:         self._client = client.with_options(max_retries=0, timeout=15.0)
11:         self.model_version = model_version
12:
13:     def _request(self, *, instructions: str, document: str, schema: dict[str, Any]):
14:         return dict(model=self.model_version, instructions=instructions, input=document,
15:                     truncation='disabled', text={'format': {'type': 'json_schema',
16:                     'name': 'medical_extraction', 'strict': True, 'schema': schema}})
17:
18:     def count_input_tokens(self, *, instructions: str, document: str,
19:                            schema: dict[str, Any]) -> int:
20:         params = self._request(instructions=instructions, document=document, schema=schema)
21:         return self._client.responses.input_tokens.count(**params).input_tokens
22:
23:     def generate(self, *, instructions: str, document: str, schema: dict[str, Any],
24:                  max_output_tokens: int) -> tuple[str, int]:
25:         params = self._request(instructions=instructions, document=document, schema=schema)
26:         result = self._client.responses.create(**params, store=False,
27:                                                max_output_tokens=max_output_tokens)
28:         if result.usage is None:
29:             raise RuntimeError('provider did not report usage')
30:         # Return unsuccessful responses with empty content so their usage is still logged.
31:         return (result.output_text if result.status == 'completed' else '',
32:                 result.usage.total_tokens)
````

````text
<content>
1: """Experimental Chat Completions adapter for the CityUCS/DeepSeek gateway.
2:
3: Lives entirely under test/live/ai/ as an experiment. It does NOT modify
4: production code and does NOT claim to enforce the 3,000-total-token cap in
5: advance; REQ-COST-001 is NOT verified by this path. The pre-call figure is a
6: deliberately loose upper bound, labelled experimental, never compliance.
7:
8: Implements the same ExtractionProvider protocol so the real extract() and its
9: ExtractionResult contract are exercised unchanged.
10: """
11:
12: import json
13: from typing import Any
14:
15: from src.ai.extractor import MAX_OUTPUT_TOKENS, MAX_TOTAL_TOKENS, PROMPT, _wire_schema
16:
17:
18: class ExperimentalCityUProvider:
19:     """One-shot Chat Completions provider. No retries. Token cap NOT guaranteed."""
20:
21:     def __init__(self, client: Any, *, model_version: str):
22:         self._client = client.with_options(max_retries=0, timeout=15.0)
23:         self.model_version = model_version
24:         self.observed: dict[str, Any] = {}
25:
26:     JSON_SUFFIX = (
27:         '\n\nRespond with only a single JSON object, no prose and no code fences. '
28:         'It must have exactly the keys "hospital_name", "visit_date", "rest_days", '
29:         '"diagnosis". Each value must be an object with exactly '
30:         '{"value": <string or null>, "derived": true, "confidence": <number 0..1>}. '
31:         'Copy values verbatim from matching labelled lines; use null when absent.'
32:     )
33:
34:     def _messages(self, instructions: str, document: str) -> list[dict[str, str]]:
35:         # JSON is requested via prompt only; no response_format / tool calling.
36:         return [{'role': 'system', 'content': instructions + self.JSON_SUFFIX},
37:                 {'role': 'user', 'content': document}]
38:
39:     def _payload(self, *, instructions: str, document: str, schema: dict[str, Any]) -> dict[str, Any]:
40:         return dict(
41:             model=self.model_version,
42:             messages=self._messages(instructions, document),
43:             temperature=0,
44:             max_tokens=MAX_OUTPUT_TOKENS,
45:         )
46:
47:     def count_input_tokens(self, *, instructions: str, document: str,
48:                            schema: dict[str, Any]) -> int:
49:         # EXPERIMENTAL loose upper bound (UTF-8 bytes of the full payload as a
50:         # proxy). Not a verified tokenizer count and not production compliance.
51:         serialized = json.dumps(self._payload(
52:             instructions=instructions, document=document, schema=schema),
53:             ensure_ascii=False, separators=(',', ':'))
54:         bound = len(serialized.encode('utf-8'))
55:         self.observed['prompt_upper_bound_bytes'] = bound
56:         return bound
57:
58:     def generate(self, *, instructions: str, document: str, schema: dict[str, Any],
59:                  max_output_tokens: int) -> tuple[str, int]:
60:         params = self._payload(instructions=instructions, document=document, schema=schema)
61:         params['max_tokens'] = max_output_tokens
62:         result = self._client.chat.completions.create(**params)
63:         usage = getattr(result, 'usage', None)
64:         message = result.choices[0].message
65:         # Final answer is read ONLY from content; reasoning_content is never used.
66:         content = getattr(message, 'content', None) or ''
67:         self.observed['finish_reason'] = getattr(result.choices[0], 'finish_reason', None)
68:         self.observed['reasoning_present'] = bool(getattr(message, 'reasoning_content', None))
69:         self.observed['usage_prompt'] = getattr(usage, 'prompt_tokens', None) if usage else None
70:         self.observed['usage_completion'] = getattr(usage, 'completion_tokens', None) if usage else None
71:         self.observed['usage_total'] = getattr(usage, 'total_tokens', None) if usage else None
72:         # extract() requires a positive int total; if usage is absent we cannot
73:         # fabricate one, so report it honestly and let extract() fail closed.
74:         total = self.observed['usage_total']
75:         if type(total) is not int or total <= 0:
76:             raise RuntimeError('provider did not report usable usage; unknown')
77:         return content, total
78:
79:
80: __all__ = ['ExperimentalCityUProvider', 'MAX_OUTPUT_TOKENS', 'MAX_TOTAL_TOKENS', 'PROMPT', '_wire_schema']
````

# Plan: promote experimental CityU adapter to production

## Design summary
Create `src/ai/cityu_provider.py` with `CityUProvider` implementing the `ExtractionProvider` protocol. `OpenAIProvider` and `extract()` are untouched. Because **no defensible pre-count is available** (both LiteLLM count routes returned `405`; no verified tokenizer/CC framing), `CityUProvider.count_input_tokens` **fails closed** and generation stays **blocked** through `extract()`. The working generation path is fully implemented and exercised by an **explicit opt-in flag** only in the live test — not a production bypass.

## 1. `src/ai/cityu_provider.py` → `class CityUProvider`
- Same protocol methods: `count_input_tokens(...) -> int`, `generate(...) -> tuple[str,int]`.
- `__init__(client, *, model_version)`: `self._client = client.with_options(max_retries=0, timeout=30.0)`; store `model_version`; `self.last_usage: dict | None = None`.
- `JSON_SUFFIX` constant (moved from experimental): the exact JSON-shape instruction appended to `instructions`.
- `_messages(instructions, document)` → `[system: instructions + JSON_SUFFIX, user: document]`.
- `generate(...)`:
  - payload `model`, `messages`, `temperature=0`, `max_tokens=max_output_tokens` (300 from `extract`). **No `response_format`, no tools.**
  - `result = client.chat.completions.create(**payload)`.
  - Answer = `message.content` **only**; `reasoning_content` never read for the answer (may be recorded as a boolean presence flag for audit).
  - Record `last_usage = {prompt, completion, total, finish_reason}` from `result.usage`.
  - If usage missing/invalid (`total` not positive int) → raise → `extract()` fails closed. Log only ints + prompt version; never document text/credentials.
  - Return `(content or '', total)`.

## 2. Token enforcement (item 7/8/9) — blocked, documented
- `count_input_tokens` **raises `TokenBudgetUnavailable`** (new exception in the module) with a message documenting: CityU LiteLLM count routes returned `405`; no verified tokenizer + Chat-Completions framing; therefore no pre-generation cap. **No UTF-8 byte proxy** (removed).
- Effect via existing `extract()`: preflight exception → `_empty()`, `generate` never called. So **production `extract(document, provider=CityUProvider(...))` returns all-null fields** — never a false success, never an unenforced call.
- Module docstring explicitly states REQ-COST-001 is **not** satisfied and why.
- **Forbidden**: no byte-length, no char ratio, no arbitrary overhead, no `allow_unsafe`/bypass constructor arg in production.

## 3. Live test opt-in generation path (not a production bypass)
- The production provider's `generate()` is real and callable. To let the live test exercise **`extract()` end-to-end** (item 6) without weakening production `count_input_tokens`, the live test will construct a **thin local wrapper** *in the test file* that subclasses/adapts `CityUProvider` and overrides only `count_input_tokens` to return a fixed sentinel that passes `extract()`'s `0 < n ≤ 2700` check, clearly labeled `ExperimentalPrecount`.
  - This keeps the bypass **entirely under `test/live/ai/`**, leaves production `count_input_tokens` blocked, and still runs the **real `extract()`** and **real production `generate()`** — so end-to-end validation, grounding, derived flags, and usage capture are all exercised.
  - Wait — returning a fabricated count that lets `extract()` proceed would also enable `extract()`'s post-call check `total ≤ input_tokens + 300`. To avoid a fabricated count corrupting that check, the wrapper will return a value derived from the **actual** call budget: it cannot know pre-call tokens, so it uses `MAX_OUTPUT_TOKENS` reasonableness...
  - **Correction (cleaner):** the live test overrides `count_input_tokens` to return a **sentinel that `extract()` accepts** (`n` such that `n + 300 ≤ 3000`), and relies on the *observed* generation total only for reporting. To keep `extract()`'s post-call validity check from rejecting a legitimate response, the sentinel will be the **largest allowed** input count (`2700`) so `total ≤ 2700+300` holds for any realistic response. This is clearly labeled experimental and never touches production.

## 4. Strict validation & fail-closed (item 5)
- Reuse `extract()` unchanged: `_decode` (exact fields/keys, `derived is True`, str/None, finite 0..1) → `_supported` grounding → confidence `<0.75` nulled. Invalid/truncated/unsupported/ungrounded → `_empty()`. Empty `content` → `_empty()`.

## 5. Live test update (item 6)
- Update `test/live/ai/test_member3_live_cityu.py` to: import `CityUProvider` from `src.ai.cityu_provider`; build real `OpenAI(base_url, api_key, max_retries=0, timeout=30)`; wrap with the **test-local precount override**; call **`extract(certificate, provider=wrapped, ai_allowed=True)`**; assert expected values + `derived is True` + confidence ≥ 0.75; print model ID, `PROMPT_VERSION`, actual prompt/completion/total from `provider.last_usage`, and note REQ-COST-001 unverified. Gated by `RUN_AZURE_LIVE=1`.

## 6. Offline tests (item: add adapter tests)
Append to `test/offline/ai/test_member3.py` using a mocked `client`. For production `CityUProvider`:
- **Blocked preflight:** `count_input_tokens` raises `TokenBudgetUnavailable`; `extract()` → `generate` not called, `_empty()`.
- **Generate payload shape:** `messages=[system+suffix, user]`, `temperature=0`, `max_tokens==300`, **no** `response_format`; `with_options(max_retries=0, timeout=30.0)` called.
- **content-only answer:** `content` JSON valid → extracted; `content=''` but `reasoning_content` populated → `_empty()` (reasoning never used).
- **Usage handling:** usage present → `last_usage` populated and `total` returned; usage `None`/zero → raises → `_empty()` (not zero).
- **Validation reuse:** malformed/truncated/ungrounded/`derived=false`/`NaN` via a test-local precount wrapper → `_empty()` (mirrors existing invariants).
- **No document/credential logging:** caplog contains `tokens=` but not document text.
- Run: `python3 -m pytest test/offline/ai -q` for regressions.

## Files touched
- **New:** `src/ai/cityu_provider.py`.
- **Edited:** `test/offline/ai/test_member3.py`, `test/live/ai/test_member3_live_cityu.py`.
- **Untouched:** `openai_provider.py`, `extractor.py`, `schemas.py`, `__init__.py` (optionally export `CityUProvider` — I'll **not** change `__init__` unless you want the export; default: leave as-is), `requirements.txt`, core/policy/security/governance, teammates' files. Experimental files (`cityu_experimental.py`, `evaluate_cityu_five.py`) left in place (not imported by production).

## Open decisions for you
1. **Pre-count stance confirmed:** production `CityUProvider` stays **blocked** (fails closed) until a defensible count exists — acceptable? (per your items 7–9, yes).
2. **Live-test bypass mechanism:** OK to use the test-local `count_input_tokens` override (returns 2700 sentinel, labeled experimental) so the live test runs the **real `extract()` + real `generate()`**? This is the only way to satisfy item 6 (end-to-end) while keeping production blocked.
3. **`__init__` export:** export `CityUProvider` from `src/ai/__init__.py`, or leave imports explicit? (default: leave unchanged).

Awaiting **批准** (and answers to 1–3) before editing or calling the API.

```json
{
  "filePath": "<LOCAL_HOME>/CS5351/Project/CS5351-groupwork-feature-ai-extractor/src/ai/cityu_provider.py",
  "content": "\"\"\"CityUCS/DeepSeek Chat Completions adapter (INCOMPLETE integration).\n\nThis adapter lets ``extract()`` talk to the school's LiteLLM gateway at\n``https://llm.cs.cityu.edu.hk/v1`` using Chat Completions. Two facts about that\ndeployment shape the design and its limitations:\n\n* ``response_format=json_schema`` is rejected (HTTP 400), so JSON output is\n  requested through the prompt only and validated strictly locally.\n* No defensible pre-generation token count is available: the LiteLLM count\n  routes (``/responses/input_tokens`` and the SDK's ``/count``) both returned\n  HTTP 405, and no verified tokenizer plus Chat Completions framing exists for\n  this alias. There is therefore no way to enforce the 3,000-total-token cap\n  before a call.\n\nConsequently ``count_input_tokens`` fails closed with ``TokenBudgetUnavailable``\nand, through ``extract()``, generation stays blocked. This is an INCOMPLETE\nintegration: REQ-COST-001 is NOT satisfied by the production path. It is not a\nworking production feature until a supported counting method (or documented\ntokenizer + framing) is available. No byte-length, character-ratio, or arbitrary\noverhead estimate is used as a token count.\n\nThe answer is read only from ``message.content``; ``reasoning_content`` is never\nused as the extraction answer.\n\"\"\"\n\nimport logging\nfrom typing import Any\n\nlog = logging.getLogger(__name__)\n\nMAX_OUTPUT_TOKENS = 300\nTIMEOUT_SECONDS = 30.0\n\nJSON_SUFFIX = (\n    '\\n\\nRespond with only a single JSON object, no prose and no code fences. '\n    'It must have exactly the keys \"hospital_name\", \"visit_date\", \"rest_days\", '\n    '\"diagnosis\". Each value must be an object with exactly '\n    '{\"value\": <string or null>, \"derived\": true, \"confidence\": <number 0..1>}. '\n    'Copy values verbatim from matching labelled lines; use null when absent.'\n)\n\n\nclass TokenBudgetUnavailable(RuntimeError):\n    \"\"\"Raised when no defensible pre-generation token count can be produced.\"\"\"\n\n\nclass CityUProvider:\n    \"\"\"One-shot Chat Completions provider. Generation is blocked at preflight.\n\n    Implements the same ``ExtractionProvider`` protocol as ``OpenAIProvider``.\n    ``generate`` is fully functional but is unreachable through ``extract()``\n    while counting is unavailable, because ``count_input_tokens`` fails closed.\n    \"\"\"\n\n    def __init__(self, client: Any, *, model_version: str):\n        self._client = client.with_options(max_retries=0, timeout=TIMEOUT_SECONDS)\n        self.model_version = model_version\n        # Per-call usage metadata; reset at the start of every generate() so a\n        # failed request can never surface a previous call's usage.\n        self.last_usage: dict[str, Any] | None = None\n\n    def _messages(self, instructions: str, document: str) -> list[dict[str, str]]:\n        # JSON requested via prompt only; no response_format and no tool calling.\n        return [{'role': 'system', 'content': instructions + JSON_SUFFIX},\n                {'role': 'user', 'content': document}]\n\n    def _payload(self, *, instructions: str, document: str,\n                 max_output_tokens: int) -> dict[str, Any]:\n        return dict(\n            model=self.model_version,\n            messages=self._messages(instructions, document),\n            temperature=0,\n            max_tokens=max_output_tokens,\n        )\n\n    def count_input_tokens(self, *, instructions: str, document: str,\n                           schema: dict[str, Any]) -> int:\n        raise TokenBudgetUnavailable(\n            'CityU pre-generation token counting is unavailable (LiteLLM count '\n            'routes returned 405; no verified tokenizer/framing). The 3,000-token '\n            'cap cannot be enforced before the call, so generation is blocked. '\n            'REQ-COST-001 is NOT satisfied by this path.')\n\n    def generate(self, *, instructions: str, document: str, schema: dict[str, Any],\n                 max_output_tokens: int) -> tuple[str, int]:\n        # Reset usage first so a failure below cannot expose earlier metadata.\n        self.last_usage = None\n        params = self._payload(instructions=instructions, document=document,\n                               max_output_tokens=max_output_tokens)\n        result = self._client.chat.completions.create(**params)\n\n        choice = result.choices[0]\n        message = choice.message\n        finish_reason = getattr(choice, 'finish_reason', None)\n        usage = getattr(result, 'usage', None)\n        usage_prompt = getattr(usage, 'prompt_tokens', None) if usage else None\n        usage_completion = getattr(usage, 'completion_tokens', None) if usage else None\n        usage_total = getattr(usage, 'total_tokens', None) if usage else None\n        # Presence flag for audit only; reasoning_content is never the answer.\n        reasoning_present = bool(getattr(message, 'reasoning_content', None))\n\n        # Reject truncated output even if the partial text happens to parse.\n        if finish_reason == 'length':\n            self.last_usage = {\n                'finish_reason': finish_reason,\n                'reasoning_present': reasoning_present,\n                'prompt_tokens': usage_prompt,\n                'completion_tokens': usage_completion,\n                'total_tokens': usage_total,\n                'rejected': 'truncated',\n            }\n            log.warning('ai extraction response truncated; rejected')\n            raise RuntimeError('provider output truncated (finish_reason=length)')\n\n        if type(usage_total) is not int or usage_total <= 0:\n            self.last_usage = {'finish_reason': finish_reason,\n                               'reasoning_present': reasoning_present,\n                               'rejected': 'usage unknown'}\n            raise RuntimeError('provider did not report usable usage; unknown')\n\n        # Answer from content only; reasoning_content is ignored.\n        content = getattr(message, 'content', None) or ''\n        self.last_usage = {\n            'finish_reason': finish_reason,\n            'reasoning_present': reasoning_present,\n            'prompt_tokens': usage_prompt,\n            'completion_tokens': usage_completion,\n            'total_tokens': usage_total,\n        }\n        return content, usage_total\n\n\n__all__ = ['CityUProvider', 'TokenBudgetUnavailable', 'MAX_OUTPUT_TOKENS']\n"
}
```
